Election/press is dead as a verifier: the incumbent has been returned with shares above 90 percent at every election since 2003 (NBC News, 2024), the 2015 referendum removed his term limit by a similar margin (Al Jazeera, 2015), and the two private weeklies best known for critical coverage of the ruling party, Umuseso and Umuvugizi, were suspended for six months in April 2010, before the presidential vote (Committee to Protect Journalists, 2010). Referendum has served only as a plebiscite. Bankruptcy is live for small firms and decorative for the party's. The state's veto is on speech, not on generation; imihigo itself is ex post scoring of completed delivery, which is verification in form, and the defect is in who reads it. Two imported verifiers stood in for the domestic ones and both have shown their limits. The World Bank was the first, a benchmark read from outside on the generator's own survey. Official surveys showed poverty falling from 44.9 percent in 2010/11 to 39.1 percent in 2013/14 and 38.2 percent in 2016/17; the Financial Times argued in August 2019, from the survey's own data, that poverty most likely rose between 2010 and 2014, because the statistics office changed the consumption basket and deflated it with a price index showing about 5 percent cumulative inflation against a consumer price index showing 23 percent, and it reported that five World Bank staff had warned internally in 2015 of manipulation of official statistics (France 24, 2019; ROAPE, 2019). The Bank's reply of April 2019 confirmed the inconsistency between the two price indices, found the 2016 methodology technically sound, stated that it had not found any clear sign of errors or manipulations, and launched a joint research program with the office whose figures were in question (World Bank, 2019). A verifier that gets its data from the generator cannot verify the data; it can verify the arithmetic, and it did. The seventh survey, released in April 2025, reports poverty at 27.4 percent in 2023/24 against a 2016/17 figure rebased to 39.8 percent under a new methodology (NISR, 2025), so the series that was disputed has been replaced by one that cannot be compared with it, which is what a benchmark under pressure looks like from outside: the ruler changes the ruler. The second, the World Bank's Doing Business index, on which Rwanda ranked 41st, 29th, and 38th in the last three editions and second in Africa in each (Rwanda Development Board, 2019), was discontinued in September 2021 over pressure exerted on behalf of a larger generator (the China case, Chapter 10); Rwanda's scores were not implicated, but the certificate it advertised itself on proved lobbyable.

**Bindingness.** Consequences bind downward and not upward. A mayor who misses a contract can be removed, and the churn of district leadership the evaluations complain of is the consequence firing; nothing binds on the party that sets the targets, produces the statistics, appoints the evaluators, and owns the papers that would otherwise print a different number. The tournament is the same design as the Chinese provincial one (the China case, Chapter 10), with the same defect in the same place, and without the export verifier that gave China a second reading at scale. The market failure of the same shape is a firm's internal target with no outside reader, the cross-selling quotas that produced millions of unauthorized accounts at Wells Fargo in the years to 2016 (Department of Justice, 2020): a metric set, read, and reported inside the generator selects for the metric and for nothing the metric cannot see.

**The component.** A benchmark with no independent reader decays by Goodhart's law: the district sets the target, a state office produces the number, a presidential team scores it, and no press can print the other figure, so the metric is optimized until it measures the optimization, and the poverty dispute of 2019 is the first reading of that decay, a benchmark dying on schedule, and a dead verifier predicts failure with near certainty (P7).

**Prediction.** If the survey microdata and price deflators behind the 2023/24 poverty figure are released for outside reanalysis, expect the figure to be revised upward within one survey cycle; if they are not, expect the next imihigo assessment to report district averages above 80 percent beside the fatigue and unrealistic projects the 2026 assessment already names, and the gap between scored delivery and measured welfare to widen within five years.

### Botswana

**Verdict.** Botswana's loop is partial and closing: the diamond rent was verified from the day it was found and the consequence bound in October 2024, when the electorate removed a party that had governed for 58 years, so the diamond slump of 2024 to 2026 is the current test of whether the fiscal verifiers hold under a shrinking rent.

**Operated and structured.** The state operates half of a mining company and structures the rest of the rent's path. Debswana is a 50:50 joint venture between the government and De Beers operating Jwaneng, Orapa, Letlhakane, and Damtshaa, with a new sales agreement and a license extension beyond 2029 concluded on 4 February 2025 (Debswana; joint announcement, 2025), under which the state's own sales company sells a rising share of the output beside De Beers (National Jeweler, 2025), a benchmark on the partner's prices built into the contract. The revenue enters through the Bank of Botswana into the Pula Fund, created in 1993 under the Bank of Botswana Act of 1975 and re-established under the 1996 Act, split into a Government Investment Account and a reserve account, and it leaves under two rules: a Sustainable Budget Index that caps recurrent spending relative to non-mineral revenue, so that diamonds fund investment and not salaries, and a ceiling on total spending at 40 percent of GDP (NRGI, 2013). Diamonds are the residual: the lens does not explain why Botswana had them, only why they did not rot it, and its small population is a multiplier that made every verifier cheaper to run. The system's own logic is the most disciplined answer to a windfall on record and should be stated as its designers would. A government that did not trust itself with the rent put the accounts in the hands of a foreign partner whose shareholders would audit them, the savings in a central bank with a published mandate, the spending under a rule readable in a single ratio, and its own tenure under an election held every five years since independence (Wikipedia, Elections in Botswana), on the theory that a rent verified at every step could not become what it became in Angola or Zaire. The state also operated where the axis allowed: the national antiretroviral program launched in 2002, the first in Africa to offer free treatment (Lancet Global Health, 2013), delivered a coverage rate a visiting team can count, and it was counted.

**Live verifiers.** Price is live and incorruptible for the output, with the honest complication that for most of the twentieth century the world diamond price was administered by the partner: De Beers ran supply through its central selling organization (Wikipedia, De Beers), a private cartel administering a price, the market failure of the same shape as a state price control, and the exit verifier that broke it was laboratory-grown stones, whose rise is among the largest causes of the slump this case ends on (Fortune, 2024). Export is live: every carat is sold abroad. Benchmark is live in the fund and the rules, with the soft spot the NRGI names, that asset-level transparency of the Pula Fund is weak (NRGI, 2013), so the rule is verified in aggregate and not in holdings. Election/press is live and, since 2024, proven: the press is among the freer on the continent (Freedom House, 2025), the courts have overturned executive acts, among them the removal of the San from the Central Kalahari Game Reserve (High Court of Botswana, 2006) and the refusal to register a gay rights group (Court of Appeal of Botswana, 2016), and the kgotla, the village assembly at which a chief hears and answers his people, is the election/press verifier in its pre-state form, Hirschman's voice, one that predates the state and that Acemoglu, Johnson, and Robinson (2003) identified as the reason the colonial and post-colonial state met an audience already used to being answered. On 30 October 2024 the Umbrella for Democratic Change won at least 31 of 61 seats, the Botswana Democratic Party fell to fourth, Masisi conceded with the words "we got it wrong big time", and Duma Boko was sworn in on 1 November, ending 58 years of one-party government by ballot (Al Jazeera, 2024; CNBC Africa, 2024). Bankruptcy is live for firms and softer for the state's own: the national airline has been bailed out repeatedly (Mmegi, 2026), the soft constraint in the sectors where verification is expensive. Referendum is not used. Verifier and veto separate cleanly here: the courts have evaluated completed government acts rather than blocked generation ex ante, and the kgotla consults before decision, a veto in form but a cheap one, a meeting rather than a review.

**Bindingness.** The slump is the test and it is running. Debswana's output fell 27 percent in 2024 to 17.9 million carats and to 15.1 million in 2025, about 40 percent below 2023 and far below a typical 24 million; GDP contracted 2.8 percent in 2024 and 0.4 percent in 2025; the fiscal deficit is 9.3 percent of GDP for 2025/26 with 8.9 percent projected for the following year; the Government Investment Account fell from BWP 5.4 billion in mid-2024 to BWP 846 million at the end of 2025; and S&P projects net debt reaching 37.4 percent of GDP by 2029 from a net-creditor position of 6.3 percent in 2023 (Ecofin Agency, 2026; Mining.com, 2026; IMF, 2025). Two readings of the same numbers are possible. The fund did its job as a buffer, which is what it was for, and the deficit is the arithmetic of a rent that fell by two fifths, not of a rule that was broken; but a buffer drawn to a sixth of its size in eighteen months is a rule at its limit, and the question the rule now poses is whether recurrent spending, the salaries the Sustainable Budget Index was written to keep off the diamond account, is cut to the non-mineral revenue or financed by the borrowing S&P has already priced. The consequence bound once: the electorate that removed the BDP did so in the first year of the slump, on a record of unemployment and a rent it judged mismanaged, and the new government inherits the same rules and the same rating agencies. Debswana plans 18 million carats in 2026 and the finance minister projects 3.1 percent growth (Mining.com, 2026), a forecast the same verifiers will grade.

**The component.** The rent was verified and the consequence bound: the diamond revenue passed through a partner's audited accounts, a central bank's published fund, a fiscal rule readable in one ratio, and an election that removed the government when the rent fell, which is the asymmetric law's positive half, live verifiers enabling a resource state to escape the curse without guaranteeing that it survives the slump (P7).

**Prediction.** If the Sustainable Budget Index and the 40 percent expenditure ceiling are held through the slump, expect the deficit below 4 percent of GDP by the 2028/29 budget with net debt short of S&P's projected 37.4 percent; if either rule is suspended for two consecutive budgets, expect a sovereign downgrade within two years and the 2029 election to be fought on the rent, as 2024 was.

## Chapter 11. Broken Loops

Botswana closed on a rent that had, so far, been verified and a consequence that had, in 2024, bound, which is the configuration the chapter now leaves behind. The systems that follow are the ones in which the verifiers were not weakened but killed, in an order that every autopsy reports the same way, and the grocery store of Chapter 1 returns here in its native form: the queue, the shelf, the ration book. The theory is a theory of failure first, and this is the chapter where it does its primary work.

### The USSR

**Verdict.** The Soviet loop was open by design from the first five-year plan in 1928 and stayed open for six decades; its trajectory was decay, masked first by mobilization and then by oil, and the dissolution of December 1991 was overdetermined, because every one of the eight verifiers had been dead at once for a generation.

**Operated and structured.** The state operated everything and structured nothing. Gosplan set targets in physical units through material balances; the branch ministries passed the targets to enterprises; Gossnab assigned each enterprise its suppliers and its customers; the State Committee on Prices, Goskomtsen, fixed the price of every good, some unchanged for decades, the retail price of bread holding from 1955 (Christian Science Monitor, 1985); Gosbank issued the credit the plan required; the state trade network sold the output at the fixed price. Nothing was bought through a verifier the state did not control, because there was never a second bidder: the supplier was assigned, so there was no tender, and the customer was assigned, so there was no exit. The plan's own logic deserves its best statement. Against a world market that in 1929 was visibly failing to coordinate anything, the plan promised coordination without the waste of duplication and speculation, and mobilization of a peasant country into an industrial one within a decade rather than a century; and it delivered, where it was pointed, on a scale no market had matched in the same time: the Urals steel base, tank production that outran Germany's by nearly three to one across 1942 and 1943 (WarHistory.org), near-universal literacy by the 1959 census (Likbez), the first satellite in October 1957. The theory's reading of that record is not that planning worked and then stopped. Every item on it is a count: a tank exists or does not, a ton of steel weighs a ton, a child can read or cannot, an orbit is observed from every continent. Those are the outputs whose verification cost is lowest, and the one that mattered most, the tank, was verified by the only verifier no state can capture, the enemy. The plan succeeded exactly where output could be counted by someone other than the generator, which is the operator rule's prediction (Chapter 3), and it failed wherever quality was not a count: a shoe that fit, a harvest, a machine tool anyone wanted, a program that ran. The record has been read for seventy years as evidence that planning works in emergencies. It is evidence that counting works, in emergencies and out of them.

The plan was also the limit case of the veto point. Nothing could be produced without an allocation and a target, so every act of generation required ex ante permission, and nothing was evaluated afterwards except by the body that had ordered it. That is vetocracy with the verification removed: verification theater at the scale of a continent, and the reason the Soviet economy could have both more paperwork per unit of output than any economy in history and no verdict on any unit of it.

**Live verifiers.** None of the eight was live for the output the plan existed to make, and the ledger is worth reading in full because the completeness is the case. Price: administered, so the tag on a Soviet shelf carried a number and no verdict, and the verdict migrated to the queue (Chapter 2). Tender: absent, one assigned supplier per input. Exit: forbidden for enterprises, which could not change suppliers, and for citizens, who held internal passports from the decree of December 1932 (Wikipedia) and could not leave the country; the customer's exit ran into the second economy, where it was a crime, and the tolkach, the enterprise's illegal expediter who obtained what Gossnab had not delivered, was the exit verifier operating as an offense. Export: partially live and revealing in what it verified. Oil, gas, gold, and timber sold at world prices, so the state knew precisely what its raw materials were worth and nothing about the value of anything it manufactured; manufactured exports to hard-currency markets were negligible, fuel and raw materials making up about four-fifths of Soviet exports in the 1980s (Carnegie Endowment, 2017), and trade within the bloc ran at negotiated prices that verified nothing. Election/press: decorative, a single candidate on the ballot and Glavlit reading every page before it was printed, so the press was the generator's own report distributed at scale. Benchmark: captured, because republics and enterprises were compared on fulfillment figures the compared units had themselves reported. Referendum: held once, on 17 March 1991, on preserving the Union, approved by about 78 percent with six republics boycotting (Wikipedia), nine months before the Union ceased to exist, which is what a referendum installed at the end of a system rather than at the beginning verifies. Bankruptcy: nonexistent; a loss-making plant was a plant with a subsidy, and no Soviet enterprise closed for losing money.

Two internal verifiers deserve naming because each shows what happens to a verifier inside an open loop. The first is science, whose verifier is the experiment. From the August 1948 session of the agricultural academy (Wikipedia), Lysenko's biology was the only biology permitted, Mendelian genetics was banned, and Vavilov, the country's leading geneticist, had already died in prison in 1943 (Wikipedia); the field reported to the patron for a generation, and the wheat failed to grow, which was the experiment delivering its verdict to a system that had stopped reading experiments (the capture of science, Chapter 15). The second is the Novosibirsk report, a paper Zaslavskaya delivered at a closed seminar in April 1983 and which reached the Western press, the Washington Post, that August (Wikipedia), arguing that the economic mechanism had become an obstacle to the society it administered. It was an accurate internal verifier, and it could not bind, because its only authorized reader was the generator it evaluated; it had the structure of an internal audit at a firm whose board is the chief executive, and it changed nothing until the loop was opened from outside.

**Bindingness.** The soft constraint (Kornai's term for a consequence that will predictably be cancelled) was not a defect of the Soviet enterprise; it was the enterprise's charter, and Kornai's shortage economy was the aggregate result. The mechanism by which the statistics were fabricated followed from the mechanism by which the consequence was cancelled. Next year's target was set from this year's reported output plus an increment, the ratchet, so a manager who reported true capacity was punished with an impossible target, and the rational manager underreported capacity, hoarded labor and inputs against the next shortage, and stormed at the end of each quarter to hit the number. The ministry that received the report needed it to be true in order to report upward, so nobody checked, and the overstatement compounded at every level. Where the target was gross output by weight, factories made heavy nails and thick glass; where it was by count, they made small ones. The Uzbek cotton affair of the 1980s, in which nearly a million tons of cotton in a single year were reported, paid for, and never grown, and Uzbekistan drew some four billion rubles for nonexistent cotton (Wikipedia), is the mechanism at republic scale. That is Goodhart decay in its purest recorded form, and it needed no ideology: the same decay is documented in a Chinese province reporting its GDP (Chapter 10) and in a hospital reporting its waiting list (Chapter 15).

The reforms confirm the diagnosis from the other side, because both tried to install a consequence without a verifier. The Kosygin reforms of 1965, following Liberman's 1962 Pravda article (Wikipedia), made profit and sales the enterprise's success indicators, let it retain part of the profit in incentive funds, and cut the centrally imposed indicators from thirty to nine; they were reversed and absorbed by the ministries between 1968 and 1972 (Wikipedia). Gorbachev's full khozraschet, in the Law on State Enterprise adopted in 1987 and in force from January 1988 (Britannica), went further: self-financing, elected managers, state orders in place of the whole plan. Neither could work, and the reason is structural rather than political. Profit computed at administered prices is not a verdict; sales to assigned customers are not exit; a consequence attached to a fabricated number selects for better fabrication. The 1987 law produced wage growth far in excess of productivity, the state bank losing control of the money supply, and a worse shortage (Britannica), which is the expected result of hardening a constraint on a generator whose reports are still the only evidence. The market has run the same experiment. A bank whose regulator forbears (the Japan case, Chapter 10) is one enterprise under Gosbank, and a firm paying its verifier for its verdict (the rating agencies case, Chapter 12) is one ministry receiving its own fulfillment report; the Soviet Union differed only in having made the arrangement universal. The grocery store, the book's calibration object, sits at the bottom of this ledger and is its plainest entry. The price on the Soviet shelf was fixed, sometimes for decades, so the true price was the queue, paid in hours; and the queue is a verifier that fired every day in every town, with a verdict that landed on the shopper and never on the shop (Chapter 3). Every Soviet citizen verified the plan daily at the cost of an afternoon, and the plan received none of it, because a queue is exit with the door locked and price with the number removed. The second market in the alley was the same verifier operating as a crime. Groceries are the cheapest output in the world to verify, and the state that ran them unverified for sixty years produced the result the lens predicts: not a bad grocer but a building with a line outside it. Oil deferred the reckoning rather than cancelling it. After 1973 hard-currency oil revenue bought the grain the collective farms did not grow, imports rising roughly twentyfold between 1970 and 1985 (Carnegie Endowment, 2017), and the price fall of 1986 removed the mask in the decade the system ended (Chapter 6); the lens does not explain why Siberia had oil, only why oil changed the date and not the direction.

**The component.** Every one of the eight verifiers was dead at once, which is why the collapse was overdetermined: not one autopsy finding but eight, and the asymmetric law makes its strongest claim, near certainty, exactly in this configuration. The collapse surprised so many observers because they were reading the generator's reports; Western estimates of Soviet output were built on Soviet official statistics (GAO, 1991), so the outside world had captured itself with the generator's ledger. The test of the diagnosis is what happened when one verifier was switched on. Glasnost freed the press from 1986 (Wikipedia) with no binding consequence attached, and the semi-competitive elections of March 1989 attached one (Wikipedia); sixty years of suppressed verdicts then arrived within five, and the system dissolved rather than reformed, because a loop that has been open for a generation has no tissue left that can read a verdict, only tissue that can suffer one. Reform requires a generator that has been taking small verdicts continuously; the Soviet generator had taken none, and the first true one was fatal. The dissolution was a reset in Olson's sense (Chapter 5), which reopened the loop and built no verifier, and what grew on the cleared ground after 1991 is the Russian case the atlas does not attempt (P3, P7).

**Prediction.** If the election/press verifier is switched on in a system whose other verifiers are dead and whose consequences are soft (Cuba and North Korea are the clearest surviving replicas), expect dissolution rather than reform within five years, on the pattern of 1986 to 1991, because the accumulated verdicts arrive faster than a price or bankruptcy verifier can be built to read them; and if instead the price and bankruptcy verifiers are restored first with the press still dead, the Chinese order after 1978 (Chapter 10), expect growth in the counted sectors for two decades and Goodhart decay of the benchmark within one.

### Cuba and Venezuela

**Verdict.** Both loops are broken, with price, exit, election/press, and bankruptcy extinguished simultaneously; Venezuela's is in an unmasking that began with the oil price in 2014 and reached the state itself in January 2026 (CRS, 2026), and Cuba's is decaying with exit finally exercised by something between a tenth and a fifth of its population, a range the state itself cannot narrow.

**Operated and structured.** Venezuela operated the oil company, the exchange rate, the price of every staple, and the shelf, and structured nothing. PDVSA, nationalized on 1 January 1976 (Wikipedia), was the state's revenue; CADIVI, the exchange control of February 2003 (Wikipedia), was a veto point on every import, an ex ante permission to buy dollars that verified nothing about what the dollars bought; the fair-price laws of 2011 and January 2014 (Gaceta Oficial, 2014) set ceilings for staples below cost (Chapter 2); Mercal, the state grocery chain founded in 2003 (Wikipedia), sold at those ceilings, and the CLAP boxes from 2016 (Wikipedia) replaced the shelf with a delivery when the shelf could no longer be stocked. Expropriation took farms, the steel maker Sidor in 2008 (Wikipedia), cement, and retail chains whose crime was selling at the price the shelf demanded. Cuba's state has operated the whole economy since the offensive of 1968 that nationalized the last street vendors (Wikipedia); the bodega has distributed the ration against the libreta since 1962 (Wikipedia); self-employment was licensed in narrow categories from 1993 and widened in 2010 (Columbia Cuba Capacity Building Project, 2022), and private small firms were legalized in 2021 (Wikipedia), each a licensing veto rather than a structured loop, since the state still set the wholesale price and owned the wholesaler. The steel-man is real in both. The Bolivarian logic was that the oil belonged to the nation and its rent should reach the shelf directly rather than through a private sector that had captured the rent for a century, and for a decade the misiones did reduce measured poverty, which fell by more than 20 percent between 2002 and 2008 (Wikipedia), and the shelf was full. Cuba's logic was that an island under embargo should ration by right rather than by wealth, and its literacy, vaccination, and infant-mortality figures sat for decades far above its income (Berdine, Geloso and Powell, 2018), which the lens reads without surprise: those are the outputs that can be counted, and the counted outputs are where every operator of an open loop performs.

**Live verifiers.** Venezuela's ledger from 2003 onward reads as follows. Price: extinguished by law, and then the ledger of the verifier was itself retired, since the central bank stopped publishing inflation in 2015 and resumed in May 2019 (Venezuelanalysis, 2019); from 2019 the dollar circulated openly (Wikipedia), restoring price for those who held dollars and confirming that the bolívar price had been a number and not a verdict. Exit: killed for capital by exchange control and for firms by expropriation, and exercised by people: seven to eight million, roughly a quarter of the population, had left by 2024 (UNHCR via Wikipedia). Export: the one live verifier, the world oil price, and it verified the oil and not the state; the verdict on PDVSA's management was written in barrels, output falling from 2.3 million barrels a day before 2014 to a fraction of that (Economics Observatory, 2024), after the 2002 to 2003 strike in which nearly 19,000 of the professional staff who had run the fields were dismissed (Wikipedia). Election/press: decorative in the precise sense Chapter 1 gives the word. In the election of 28 July 2024 the electoral council declared Maduro the winner without publishing precinct results, opposition-collected tally sheets from about 80 percent of precincts showed González with 67 percent, and the Carter Center said it could not verify or corroborate the official result (Carter Center, 2024; CRS, 2024). A count that is not published is not a verifier, and a press that cannot print the count is not a press; the verdict existed, on paper, in the opposition's hands, and it could be overridden at will, which is the definition of decoration. Benchmark: withheld, since the statistical office stopped comparing anything. Bankruptcy: none, PDVSA being the entity that could not fail. Cuba's ledger is shorter because there was less to kill. Price: administered until the Tarea Ordenamiento of 1 January 2021 unified the two pesos and devalued the official rate, after which official inflation ran 77 percent in 2021, 39 percent in 2022, and 31 percent in 2023, with output in 2024 more than 10 percent below 2018 and the peso having lost about 88 percent of its parallel-market value (Columbia Cuba Capacity Building Project, 2025; BTI, 2026). The unification did not create the inflation; it priced sixty years of it. Exit: exercised on a scale the state cannot measure. The official population fell below 10 million by July 2024, 10.1 percent below December 2020; United States data record 738,680 Cuban arrivals between October 2021 and April 2024; an independent demographer estimates 1.79 million departures in 2021 to 2024 and a resident population near 8.6 million against the official 9.7 million (University of Navarra, 2025; CiberCuba, 2025). The state does not know how many people it governs, which is a verifier failure about the verifier. Export: nickel, medical services, tourism (BTI, 2026), all small; the largest hard-currency inflow is remittances, which is exit paying for those who stayed. Election/press: decorative. Bankruptcy: none. And one verifier the state did not install and cannot report away, the grid: nationwide collapses on 18 to 22 October 2024, 6 November 2024, 4 December 2024, 11 September 2025, 3 December 2025, and 4, 16, and 21 March 2026 (Wikipedia, 2026). A blackout is a verdict of near-zero cost; the whole island reads it at once, and it is the one the ministry has never been able to override.

**Bindingness.** PDVSA is the firm under the softest constraint on record, and it is the market-side half of this case. Its owner could not let it fail, took its cash for the misiones and its board for politics, replaced its engineers with loyalists, and covered its losses by having the central bank take its paper (International Banker, 2017), so that the firm's losses became the currency's; a zombie firm under a forbearing bank (the Japan case, Chapter 10; the Japanese zombie banking case, Chapter 12) is the same object at the scale of one balance sheet, and the difference is that a bank eventually runs out of capital and a central bank does not, which is why the Venezuelan version ran until the currency was worth nothing rather than until a lender said no. The order in which the verifiers died matches the sequence Chapter 15 states: bindingness first (exchange control in 2003 (Wikipedia) made every loss recoverable from the oil account), independence second (the court and the electoral council, packed from 2004 (Wikipedia)), and cost last (the statistics retired in 2015). The Venezuelan shelf is the grocery store's second appearance in this chapter, and it is the Soviet queue with the oil removed. Mercal sold at the fixed price while it had stock; when the stock failed, purchase days were rationed by identity-card number (Fox News, 2014), the queue formed, and the bachaquero, the reseller who stood in the queue and sold the controlled goods at the true price on the sidewalk, was exit and price operating as a crime, exactly the tolkach's role. The shelf was the ledger the central bank had stopped keeping. Cuba's Tarea Ordenamiento was the Soviet reform pattern repeated: a consequence installed without a verifier. It unified the exchange rate and put state enterprises on cost accounting, but with no exit for capital and no bankruptcy for a state enterprise, so the price rose and the shelf did not fill; the state then opened stores selling in freely convertible currency, from 2019 for appliances and 2020 for food (Cuba Study Group, 2022), a currency it does not issue and its population obtains from emigrants, which is the state grocer's written confession that its own price is not a price. The bodega and the libreta are the limit of the fixed price: a price of zero with a quantity attached, the quantity shrinking for decades (Wikipedia) and the difference made up at the informal price the state does not set. The private version of this shape is the Canadian plaza whose covenant forbids a second grocer (Chapter 12); the bodega is the covenant with no plaza left to exit to.

**The component.** Oil rent kept the loop open for a decade without visible failure. Between 2004 and 2014 (Economics Observatory, 2024) the shelf was full, the misiones ran, and the verdicts of four dead verifiers were deferred rather than cancelled; when the price fell, all of them arrived at once, and GDP per head, the plainest measure of living standards, fell by about three quarters between 2013 and the early 2020s, the fifth-largest collapse in modern economic history (Economics Observatory, 2024). That is the mask mechanism the asymmetric law names (Chapter 6): a rent can pay the running cost of an open loop but cannot read a verdict, and when the rent stops, the loop is exactly as open as it was on the first day. Cuba wore three masks in sequence, Soviet subsidy until 1991, Venezuelan oil until the mid-2010s (AS/COA, 2016), and remittances since, and the blackouts are what the third mask cannot cover. The lens does not explain why Venezuela had oil or Cuba an embargo, only why neither altered the direction. The events of 2026 belong here and not under repair. According to the Congressional Research Service, Maduro was captured by United States forces on 3 January 2026, the vice-president became acting president and moved toward cooperation with Washington, the United States took control of oil-sales revenue, an amnesty law and prisoner releases followed, the opposition's candidate and its leader remain in exile, two earthquakes on 24 June 2026 killed more than 6,100 people by the count of mid-August, and dialogue opened on 6 August 2026 with no election date set (CRS, 2026). On the ledger this is an unmasking: the oil account has been moved outside the generator by force rather than by design, the price verifier is partly live through the dollar, and the election/press verifier remains exactly as decorative as on 28 July 2024, since no count has been published and no date exists on which one would be. A repair would be a published precinct-level count and a bankruptcy verifier on PDVSA, and as of August 2026 neither exists. The failure was overdetermined and predictable in advance because four verifiers were killed at once; the unmasking was predictable from the oil price alone (P3, P7).

**Prediction.** If Venezuela's next election publishes precinct-level results and the oil revenue, now held outside the ministry's reach in United States Treasury accounts whose figures have not been disclosed (CFR, 2026), is moved into a published account, expect shelves stocked at market prices and food output recovering within three years without the oil price returning to its 2012 level; if either condition fails, expect the recovery to track the oil price alone and the shelf to empty again within one commodity cycle, and expect Cuba, where neither condition is in prospect, to record its next nationwide grid collapse before it records a resident population it can verify.

### Argentina

**Verdict.** The loop is open and has been for most of a century; since December 2023 (Milei inaugurated 10 December 2023, Wikipedia) it has been closing on the two legs that were most completely dead, price and bindingness, and whether it stays closed is pending.

**Operated and structured.** The steel-man is the Peronist settlement itself. A commodity exporter whose income arrives through the world price of grain and beef has a reasonable objection to letting the Chicago wheat pit set the living standard of Buenos Aires, and the machine built from the 1940s (the Perón settlement, with IAPI capturing export income, Wikipedia) converted export income into urban wages, protected industry, and a state that arbitrated between them. The machine was a set of distributional coalitions in Olson's exact sense: unions with a statutory monopoly over bargaining (the personería gremial, granted to one union per activity under Law 23,551, Castro Sammartino and Pierini, 2020), industries behind tariff walls, provinces financed by federal transfers (coparticipación, the sharing of nationally collected taxes under Law 23,548 of 1988, Buenos Aires Times, 2026), each holding a rent and each able to block its removal. What the state operated changed by decade (oil, railways, airlines, and utilities nationalized, sold in the 1990s, partly renationalized after 2008 (YPF, 51 percent, May 2012, Wikipedia)), and the ownership oscillation is not the case. The case is what the coalitions did to the verifiers. Each rent was paid, when taxes and export receipts fell short, by the central bank printing for the treasury, and the inflation that followed was treated as a disease to be suppressed rather than as the verdict it was: price controls, then a fixed exchange rate, then, when those failed, a falsified index. The current program inverts the order. It operates less and its central instruments are signal interventions in Chapter 3's sense: a fiscal surplus, a currency band, a claimed end to monetary financing, and a bill that would write the ban into the central bank's charter (Central Banking, 2026).

**Live verifiers.** Price is the suppressed verifier. Inflation in Argentina is the price system reporting, monthly and to every household, that the treasury is being financed by the printing press, and for decades every government treated the report as the problem. Election/press has been live since 1983 and binding, since governments have been removed at the polls, but it is noisy with respect to fiscal policy in the sense Chapter 3 defined: the marginal voter chose between coalitions offering distribution, and no election before 2023 was won on a promise to stop printing. Export is live and incorruptible, the world price of soybeans being beyond any Argentine lobby, but the state intercepts the verdict at the port through export taxes (35 percent on soybeans from 2007 and still 24 percent in 2026, Chequeado, 2026; Infobae, 2026), so it reaches the treasury rather than the farmer. Exit for capital was disabled by controls from October 2011 (the cepo, Wikipedia) and mostly restored for retail holders in April 2025 (Bloomberg, 2025); exit for people has been the standing verdict of the professional class. Bankruptcy for the sovereign is decorative: the country has defaulted nine times since 1816 (Wikipedia) and been refinanced each time, most recently through a USD 20 billion IMF facility in April 2025 and a USD 20 billion swap line with the US Treasury in late 2025 (Bloomberg, 2025; Americas Quarterly, 2026), and the institution that censured the country's statistics is the institution that refinances its treasury, a verifier and a soft constraint (Kornai) in one body. Benchmark, the statistical apparatus, was captured outright: from 2007 to 2015 the government intervened in INDEC and the published inflation index ran far below private and provincial estimates, private economists near 25 percent against an official 10.8 percent in the disputed years (Taipei Times, 2013), until the IMF issued a declaration of censure, the first in its history against a member for the quality of its data, in February 2013 (IMF, 2013). Tender and referendum do no work in the case.

**Bindingness.** The soft constraint sat between the treasury and the central bank, and every other softness descended from it: a coalition whose rent will be printed if it is not taxed has no reason to accept a verdict from anyone. The program's claim since 2024 is zero monetary financing (Funds Society, 2026). The surplus that makes the claim possible is real on a cash basis and contested on an accrual one: a primary surplus of 1.8 percent of GDP and a financial surplus of 0.3 in 2024, then 1.4 and 0.2 in 2025, the first back-to-back financial surpluses since 2008, with capitalized interest on treasury bonds recorded below the line, so that an accrual treatment would show a deficit (Buenos Aires Herald, 2026). The inflation path is the reading the restored verifier gives: 211.4 percent in 2023, 117.8 in 2024, 31.5 in 2025 (INDEC via MercoPress, 2025; Buenos Aires Herald, 2026); the July 2026 print was 2.1 percent monthly and 33.8 annual, above expectations, and the first four months of 2026 had already accumulated 11.4 percent against a budget assumption of 10.1 for the whole year (BBVA Research, 2026; MFAT, 2026). The path is not monotonic and the case does not pretend it is. The crawling peg was replaced on 11 April 2025 by a managed float inside a band of ARS 1,000 to 1,400 per dollar, the band edges indexed to inflation from January 2026, with a reserve purchase program of up to USD 10 billion; whether this is a float is contested, since the bank intervenes at the edges and buys inside them (Bloomberg, 2025; Funds Society, 2026). The midterms of October 2025 gave the government 40.8 percent of the vote for deputies and 92 seats plus allies, past the one-third threshold that sustains vetoes and decrees, on turnout among the lowest since 1983 (Buenos Aires Herald, 2025; CNBC, 2025). What the program has not secured is the binding leg itself: as of late July 2026 the charter bill had not passed Congress (Central Banking, 2026), so the ban on financing the treasury is a promise made by the generator that broke it, and a promise is a soft constraint by definition. The market instance of the same shape sits inside the coalition: the electronics assembly regime of Tierra del Fuego (Law 19,640 of 1972, MercoPress), private firms with a rent written into statute, and the 2001 freeze on private bank deposits (the corralito, Wikipedia), when the private sector's exit was cancelled to save the sovereign's.

**The component.** The statistic was captured, so no verifier could read the economy. Between 2007 and 2015 the price verifier ran in every shop as it always had and the reading of it was forged at the source, which disconnected every consequence that depended on the reading: inflation-indexed bonds paid coupons on the false index, a transfer from bondholders to the treasury; wage settlements were anchored to it; poverty counts were computed from it; and the electorate, which compared the official figure with the price of bread each month, learned that the state's numbers were not evidence, a lesson that outlasts the government that taught it and raises the verification cost of every statistic published since. A captured verifier is worse than a dead one. A dead verifier reports nothing and the system knows it is blind; a captured one reports the opposite of the truth with the authority of an office, which is why the asymmetric law treats it as dead. The Argentine state did not fail as an owner or a spender; it failed as the captor of its own statistic, which is state failure as verifier failure, the same failure Chapter 12 finds in a verifier paid by the issuer it rates (P3, P7).

**Prediction.** If the charter ban on central bank financing of the treasury passes and INDEC's independence survives the 2027 presidential election (24 October 2027, Wikipedia), expect annual inflation below 20 percent by the end of 2027 and the accrual gap in the fiscal accounts to close as capitalized interest is refinanced at falling rates; if either is reversed, the bill failing in Congress or the statistical agency intervened again, expect monthly inflation above 5 percent within 24 months of the reversal and the cycle resumed, because the coalitions that write their own verdict have been outvoted, not dissolved.

### Greece before 2010

**Verdict.** The loop was open from euro entry to 2009, its benchmark verifier captured and its bankruptcy verifier made decorative by the currency union; it was closed in 2010 by a verifier outside the country, and the partial loop that has run since depends on that verifier staying imported.

**Operated and structured.** The steel-man is the euro bet. Greece adopted a currency Athens could not print, an interest rate set in Frankfurt, and a fiscal rule, the 3 percent deficit reference of the Stability and Growth Pact, on the premise that an imported discipline would substitute for a domestic one the polity had not built; every Mediterranean member made the same bet, and the convergence of Greek bond yields toward German, the ten-year spread narrowing from over 1,100 basis points in early 1998 to between 10 and 30 from late 2002 to 2007 (Bank of Greece, 2011), was the market agreeing. What the state operated was extensive and unverified: hospitals whose arrears were carried off the books, public enterprises whose losses went to the treasury, a pension system with retirement provisions among the most generous in the European Union (Wikipedia). What it structured was the reporting. The National Statistical Service sat inside the finance ministry, the ministry produced the number Eurostat received, and the generator therefore supplied its own verdict on the one metric the union read.

**Live verifiers.** Benchmark was captured. The Maastricht deficit criterion is a benchmark verifier in Chapter 3's sense, member states compared on one published metric, and its independence was exactly as good as the source of the number. The audit of 2004 revised the deficits for 1997 to 2003 upward, put the entry-qualifying year 1999 at 3.07 percent, and listed eleven methodological violations, though 0.7 to 1.0 points of the revision reflected the accounting change from ESA79 to ESA95 (Eurostat, 2004; OECD, 2005). In 2009 the deficit for 2008 went from 5.0 percent in the April notification to 7.7 in October, and the projection for 2009 from 3.7 to 12.5; the Commission's report of 8 January 2010 found methodological weaknesses at the statistical service, the accounting office, and the finance ministry, along with poor cooperation and an institutional setting that could not guarantee freedom from political interference, and itemized hospital arrears, the treatment of swaps, overestimated tax revenue, and misrecorded EU grants (Commission/Eurostat, 2010). Eurostat's final figure for 2009 was 15.4 percent, published on 15 November 2010 (Eurostat via Dow Jones, 2010). Election/press was live and binding. Governments changed in 2004 and 2009, and each incoming government revealed its predecessor's number, which is the verifier working at exactly the resolution Chapter 3 assigned it: it verifies a government once, at the changeover, and cannot verify a quarterly deficit, so the number was reset at each election and recaptured between them. Price, in the form of the sovereign bond spread, was dulled rather than captured: lenders believed no euro member would be allowed to default, the spread converged, and the verdict was deferred. Bankruptcy was decorative for the same reason; the no-bailout clause (Article 125 TFEU) was on paper and believed by nobody who lent. Export was suppressed by the union itself: the drachma devaluation had been the old verifier of Greek competitiveness (the last, of 12.3 percent, on entry into the exchange-rate mechanism on 16 March 1998, IMF, 1998), the euro removed it, and the tradeable sector was too small to run export discipline in Studwell's sense. Exit for capital was free inside the euro and was the channel through which the verdict finally arrived. The private verifiers failed in the same shape, which is the symmetry the chapter owes: the rating agencies held Greece at single-A until the downgrades of December 2009 (TheGlobalEconomy), and the currency swap Goldman Sachs arranged for Greece in 2001 was an instrument sold by a private generator to a public one that reduced the debt the benchmark reported; the Commission's 2010 report lists the treatment of swaps among the misrecordings (European Commission, 2010).

**Bindingness.** The soft constraint (Kornai's term) was the union. The Pact's penalties had been shown to be unenforced against the large when Germany and France breached the reference value and the Council held the procedure in abeyance in November 2003, a decision the Court of Justice annulled in July 2004 (EIoP, 2005), and the European Central Bank accepted Greek bonds as collateral on terms that did not distinguish them from German (Sibert, 2010). A rule everyone knows will not be enforced is not a consequence; it is a benchmark with no promotion or demotion attached. When the consequence arrived it arrived a decade late and landed on the wrong party: three programs disbursed EUR 288.7 billion between 2010 and August 2018 (ESM, 2018), and the conditionality bound a public that had verified nothing rather than the officials who had written the number. Bindingness has been imported since, through program conditionality and then through the surveillance that followed it, and the union has meanwhile rewritten its own benchmark, adopting net primary expenditure as the single operational indicator in April 2024 (Council of the EU, 2024), a change whose bindingness is untested.

**The component.** The verifier that fired was outside the country. Every domestic instrument was in place and inert: an elected parliament, a free press, a court system, a statistical service, and an auditor, and none of them produced the true number, because each was either the generator or reported to it. Eurostat produced it, with on-site inspection powers strengthened by Regulation 679/2010 (EU, July 2010), and ELSTAT was created the same year as an independent authority (Law 3832/2010) with Andreas Georgiou as president from 2010 to 2015. What the domestic system then did with an honest verifier is the detail that carries the case. Georgiou was charged in 2013 with inflating the 2009 deficit, 15.4 percent against the 13.6 his critics claimed; he was acquitted of that charge three times, most recently by the Council of Appeals on 1 March 2019 after the Supreme Court prosecutor twice revoked earlier acquittals; in 2017 he received a two-year suspended sentence for breach of duty, upheld on appeal in 2018, for not submitting the figures to the ELSTAT board before transmitting them, and a separate civil defamation judgment went against him over statements defending the data (National Herald, 2019; ISI, 2019). After the European Court of Human Rights found in March 2023 that his trial on the breach-of-duty charge had been unfair, the case was reopened and the Supreme Court annulled that conviction and acquitted him in 2026 (Ta Nea, 2026); it had already annulled the appeal judgment upholding the defamation verdict in May 2023, leaving that case open to retrial (Amstat News, 2023). The charge that stuck until 2026 was procedural: not that the number was wrong but that it reached the verifier without passing through the board, which is the veto point deployed against the verifier, ex ante procedure invoked to punish an ex post verdict. The Greek case is the imported verifier hypothesis of Chapter 6 doing work outside the Gulf: the loop closed only when a verifier the generator could not reach was allowed to read the books, and it had to be imported because the domestic verifier sat inside the ministry whose deficit it counted, which is state failure as verifier failure and nothing more exotic; the asymmetric law's prediction that a captured benchmark ends in failure held (P3, P7).

**Prediction.** If Eurostat's power to inspect Greek public accounts on site remains and ELSTAT's statutory independence holds, expect no Greek annual deficit to be revised by more than two points of GDP over the next decade; if the inspection power is withdrawn or ELSTAT is returned to the finance ministry, expect a revision exceeding three points of GDP within two electoral cycles, because every domestic verifier that would catch it has already shown what it does with a true number.

### India

**Verdict.** The loop was open from 1951 to 1991 despite a live election/press verifier, and has been partial and closing since 1991. This is the first stress test. The bar from Chapter 8: the theory survives only if the noisy verifier and vetocracy were defined before India rather than for India, which they were, the noisy verifier in Chapter 3 and the veto point in Chapter 4, and the case below uses both as defined there and adds no term to either.

**Operated and structured.** The steel-man is the Second Plan's logic (the Mahalanobis model, Second Five-Year Plan of 1956, Wikipedia). In an economy short of capital and foreign exchange, an unlicensed private sector would build what was profitable rather than what the plan required, duplication would waste the scarcest input, and a license would ration capacity to the sectors that mattered; coordination under scarcity is a defensible aim and the planners who held it were not fools. The state operated the commanding heights, steel, heavy engineering, coal, and from 1969 the banks (fourteen nationalized on 19 July 1969, Wikipedia), and it structured everything else through the license. Under the Industries (Development and Regulation) Act of 1951 (Wikipedia) no undertaking above a threshold could be established, expanded, relocated, or switched to a new product without one; the Monopolies and Restrictive Trade Practices Act of 1969 (in force June 1970, ipleaders) added a second clearance for large houses; the Foreign Exchange Regulation Act of 1973 (in force January 1974, Wikipedia) a third for anything involving foreign capital or technology. Every unit of generation required ex ante permission. The verb "structured" barely applies, because the state did not design a verifier for others to generate against; it built a gate and stood at it, and a gate is Chapter 4's veto point in its purest form, a license that must be held before the work is done and is never withdrawn on a verdict about the work.

**Live verifiers.** Election/press was live and binding. General elections were held from 1952 (the first ran October 1951 to February 1952, Wikipedia), a national government was removed at the polls in 1977 (Wikipedia), and the press was free by any comparative standard; every one of the three questions from Chapter 1 gets a good answer for this verifier, and the theory's problem is that the answers did not produce growth. The resolution is the one Chapter 3 gave before India was reached. A vote is cast for a bundle of several thousand outputs, the marginal voter's bundle was decided by language, caste, the price of grain, and war, and extracting a verdict on industrial licensing from that bundle cost more than any party would pay; the verifier resolved regimes and could not resolve a licensing regime. Every other verifier was either dead or unreachable. Price existed in the bazaar and was absent at the gate: goods that were produced had prices, but entry was vetoed, so price selected among incumbents the ministry had already chosen, which is Hirschman's lazy monopoly with a permit. Export was disabled by design, with tariffs in the late 1980s of 123 percent on intermediates, 115 on capital goods, and 129 on consumer goods (PIIE, 2021), so the world price never reached the domestic firm. Exit was disabled because a second generator needed a license too, and the Ambassador car sold in nearly the same body from 1957 to 2014 (Wikipedia) and the multi-year wait for a telephone connection (2.5 million on the waiting list in 1990 and a wait of almost five years, Rajagopalan, 2022) were the visible form of a customer with nowhere to go. Bankruptcy was decorative: sick firms were carried by public banks and, from 1985, nursed by the Board for Industrial and Financial Reconstruction (the Sick Industrial Companies Act 1985, the board functional from 1987, Wikipedia), which existed to prevent the verdict rather than deliver it. Benchmark between states was weak, and tender and referendum did no work. The ledger reads: one live verifier whose resolution was national and whose object was the regime, and seven that either were dead or never received an output, because no output could be generated without permission. Growth over the period averaged the 3 to 4 percent that the country's own economists named the Hindu rate (PIIE, 2021). The case therefore does not contradict the asymmetric law; it is the law read precisely. A single live verifier at the top of the axis, verifying the wrong output type, is not a closed loop, and the theory never said it was.

What removed the Raj in 1991 was not a verdict from the electorate but a balance-of-payments crisis and the demolition of veto points. The Rao and Singh government of July 1991 devalued the rupee, abolished industrial licensing for most industries, and began cutting the tariffs above (PIIE, 2021), under an external anchor in the form of an IMF program and a World Bank structural adjustment loan of November 1991 (Wikipedia); growth accelerated to around 6 percent and above. Chapter 5 already recorded why this simplification held where the American tax code's did not: the firms freed from the license were at the same moment exposed to price and export, so the thicket had verifiers standing where it would otherwise have regrown. Two instruments have been installed since, and the first is carried forward in Chapter 17. The Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code of 2016 is a bankruptcy verifier built to be time-bound, with a statutory limit of 330 days from admission to resolution, and it fires: in the fiscal year to March 2025, 1,300 corporate debtors were rescued through resolution plans yielding about INR 3.99 lakh crore, roughly 170 percent of liquidation value and about 33 percent of admitted claims, a 67 percent haircut that is a verdict by any definition, while 43 percent of closed cases ended in liquidation (IBBI via IBC Laws, 2025). The Goods and Services Tax of 1 July 2017 is a signal unification: one tax read through one invoice chain replaced a thicket of state levies and interstate checkpoints, and the September 2025 collapse of its four main slabs into two, 5 and 18 percent with a 40 percent demerit rate (TaxTMI, 2025), thinned the signal further. Both are instruments the loop can read.

**Bindingness.** The consequence binds in manufacturing and in the tradeable services that grew after 1991, where price, export, and exit now operate, and it is soft in three places the case names. First, the bankruptcy verifier's timing leg is decaying: average duration of closed cases was 713 days overall and 853 days for cases closed in the 2025 fiscal year, against the 330-day limit (IBBI via IBC Laws, 2025), and a verdict delivered at two and a half times its statutory delay is on the way to being expensive enough to retire, which Chapter 15 identifies as the last stage of a verifier's death; a 2025 amendment bill is before Parliament (PRS, 2025) and the direction it takes is the thing to watch. Second, the market-side softness, which the symmetry rule requires and the case would need anyway: the large family conglomerates borrowed from public-sector banks whose owner was the state and whose losses the state covered, a soft constraint (Kornai's term) on private generators as complete as any on a state enterprise, producing the stock of bad loans that peaked in the late 2010s (gross bad loans of 11.6 percent of bank advances in March 2018, Business Standard, 2018) and the pattern in which a promoter lost money without losing control; the Code's bar on defaulting promoters bidding for their own firms (Section 29A, inserted November 2017, Mondaq) is the specific consequence the old regime lacked, and the recovery rate, 36.6 percent under the Code in the 2025 fiscal year against 31.5 percent under the older secured-creditor route (IBBI via IBC Laws, 2025), is the measurable difference the consequence makes. Third, the veto points that survived 1991. Land acquisition remains a slow ex ante process, and the labor provision requiring government permission before a firm above a headcount threshold can lay off workers (100 under the Industrial Disputes Act, raised to 300 by the 2020 labor codes, Wikipedia) is the License Raj in miniature, a gate at the size at which a firm would otherwise grow, and the scarcity of large firms in the Indian firm-size distribution, a distribution that in fact declines smoothly from a mass of tiny firms rather than showing the bimodal missing middle of legend (Hsieh and Olken, 2014), is the shape a veto point leaves on a sector.

The honest residual is the statistical verifier, and it is a warning rather than a verdict. The rebasing of 2015 produced a back-series dispute in which the National Statistical Commission's own committee draft was disowned as experimental; a former chief economic adviser argued in 2019 that growth from 2011 to 2017 had been about 4.5 percent rather than the official near 7, and the Prime Minister's council rebutted him as cherry-picking (The Week, 2019; Business Standard, 2019). The census due in 2021 was postponed and is now scheduled for enumeration in February 2027 (Deccan Herald, 2025), which means every per-capita statistic in the country is being computed on a denominator sixteen years old. A delayed count and a contested series are the early signs of a benchmark verifier under pressure from the generator it grades, the same pressure the China case (Chapter 10) traces to its end. The partial answer is that the new GDP series of February 2026, base year 2022 to 2023, is built on GST, payment-system, and corporate-registry administrative data with double deflation (MoSPI, 2026), and administrative data are harder to falsify than surveys because they are generated as a byproduct of transactions the state did not design for the purpose of being measured; the series shows 7.2 and 7.1 percent for the two most recent fiscal years, and whether the administrative anchors hold against political demand is the test. The residuals get their clause: scale, the linguistic federation, and the inheritance of a common-law court system are multipliers the lens does not claim, and none of them moved the growth rate until the gate was removed.

**The component.** The verifiers were present but noisy, and the veto points were dense enough that no generation reached the verifier. The one verifier that fired resolved regimes and not licensing regimes, so its verdicts were correct and irrelevant, and the license office in front of every factory ensured that the output the other seven verifiers might have judged was never produced. What grew in the space between them was the licensing bureaucracy itself, a generator whose output, the correct allocation of national capacity, could be verified by nobody and which therefore produced process, more of it each year, exactly as the sixth proposition says a unit in the dark must; the Raj was not a policy that failed but a thicket that grew where nothing could see, and it was removed by demolition rather than by verdict because a veto point has no verifier of its own (P3, P6).

**Prediction.** If the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code keeps its promoter bar and its resolution timelines are brought back toward the statutory 330 days, expect the recovery rate to hold above the secured-creditor alternative and the public-bank bad-loan cycle not to return within a decade; if the 2025 amendment or its successors soften the consequence so that defaulting promoters routinely retain control and average duration keeps lengthening, expect recovery rates to fall back below the 31.5 percent of the older route within five years and a renewed stock of bank losses on the conglomerates' books within ten, because a bankruptcy verifier that is slow and overridable is a decorative one and the soft constraint that preceded it never left the banks.

### North Korea

**Verdict.** Broken, all eight verifiers dead, and stable only in the sense that nothing remains to decay; the case is the limit against which every other entry in this chapter is measured.

**Operated and structured.** The state operates everything it acknowledges and structures nothing, and the regime's own logic is that a besieged economy must be commanded, since a market under sanctions would price the siege into every household; the plan is the generator, the party is the verifier of the plan, and the same office holds both roles, which is the configuration Chapter 3 says cannot verify anything.

**Live verifiers.** Price administered, tender absent, exit a capital crime, export confined to what sanctions and a single neighbor permit, election/press a plebiscite in the exact sense Chapter 3 gave the word, benchmark falsified where it exists at all, referendum unavailable, bankruptcy inconceivable for a state that is the only enterprise.

**Bindingness.** No consequence attaches to any output, so the verdict arrived in the only form left, which is the world's: the famine of the mid-1990s, with deaths estimated anywhere from roughly 240,000 to several million and the regime having published none (Wikipedia), arrived after the Soviet subsidy that had masked the open loop was withdrawn in 1991 (Wikipedia).

**The component.** The price verifier reappeared from below, and the famine that preceded it was the asymmetric law delivered without an intermediary: a state that had killed every verifier failed by the mechanism the chapter has traced in every case before it. The jangmadang, the informal markets that grew out of the famine when the public distribution system stopped distributing (Wikipedia), are price forming where the state has abolished it, verified by every household that buys rice at the market rate rather than the ration price, and the regime's 2009 currency redenomination that wiped out market savings was an attempt to kill the verifier that the state then had to abandon within weeks (PIIE, 2010); a state with every verifier dead has one growing on its underside, because the cost of verification for groceries is zero and the demand for a verdict does not go away when the verifier is banned (P3, P7).

**Prediction.** If the regime again attempts to extinguish the jangmadang by confiscatory redenomination or a market ban, expect a market-rate food-price spike and a retreat by the state within a year, as in 2009 (PIIE, 2010); if the markets are instead tolerated into a licensed layer, expect them to become the first sector in the country with a live loop and the one the state cannot then close without famine.

## Chapter 12. Market Self-Sclerosis

The jangmadang is a price verifier growing through the floor of a state that abolished it, and the chapter that follows shows the reverse: private systems, under private title, with every legal instrument of a market at hand, in which the verifier was killed from inside. Nobody in these cases is a socialist. The intermediary who stands between the patient and the price, the rating agency paid by the issuer, the covenant on a land title that forbids the next store, the bank that rolls a dead loan, and the referee who is never held to a verdict are the same object as Gosplan, and the atlas ends on them so that no reader can leave Part II believing the lens has a side.

### US Health Insurance

**Verdict.** Open loop, decaying, and opened by intermediation rather than by ownership: the most private of the rich health systems is the one in which the patient reads no price, cannot leave, and cannot tell whether the care was good, and it has behaved for four decades exactly as a system with those three facts must behave.

**Operated and structured.** The state operates almost nothing. It runs the veterans' hospitals directly, a small share of the whole (Veterans Health Administration, 2026), sets administered prices for the elderly and the poor through Medicare and Medicaid, and otherwise structures: it exempts employer-paid premiums from income and payroll tax, licenses providers, and since 2010 mandates the purchase of a product whose content it specifies. Everything else, the insurer, the hospital, the physician group, the pharmacy benefit manager, the billing contractor on either side of every claim, is private, and much of it is for profit. The steel-man is real. Insurance exists to remove price at the moment of illness, because a person having a stroke cannot shop and a person with cancer cannot pay from income, so pooling risk across the healthy is the only way the sick are treated at all; the employer group solves the adverse selection that kills individual insurance markets, buys at scale, and negotiates with hospitals on the employee's behalf in a way no individual could. The tax exclusion that binds coverage to the job was not designed. It was an accident of wartime wage controls, when firms competing for labor under a pay freeze offered health benefits instead and the revenue authorities ruled the benefit untaxed in 1943, a treatment codified in the Internal Revenue Code of 1954, and the accident became the largest single tax expenditure in the federal budget (Congressional Research Service, 2010). Every layer that has accumulated since has a defensible origin, and that is the point: a loop is not opened by a villain, and this one was opened by a sequence of reasonable answers to the previous layer's problem.

The stack as it stands is five generators deep before a patient meets a physician. The employer chooses a plan its employees will not choose; the insurer builds a network the patient cannot see into; the pharmacy benefit manager sets a formulary and negotiates rebates whose size is confidential and whose revenue rises with the list price it is supposed to be pushing down (Federal Trade Commission interim staff report, 2024); the provider network sets a negotiated rate that is secret to the patient by contract; the billing layer, on both sides, exists to dispute the other side's coding. Each layer's output is the next layer's input. Not one of them produces an output the patient can read, compare, or refuse, and the patient is the only party in the chain whose interest is that the care be good and cheap. Structured, in the book's sense, it is not: structuring means arranging a loop for others to generate inside, and what has been arranged here is a sequence of generators each verified, if at all, by the generator upstream.

**Live verifiers.** Price at the point of consumption is dead, not weak. Out-of-pocket spending was USD 556.6 billion of USD 5.3 trillion in 2024, 11 percent (CMS, 2024 data), so the party who could verify reads a number for one dollar in nine and reads it after the fact. The posted price is decorative: the hospital's chargemaster is a list nobody pays, the negotiated rate is confidential, and when the federal transparency rule required the negotiated rate to be published, 65 percent of 3,764 hospitals reviewed by mid-2025 had received a warning or a corrective-action request and three quarters of reviewed hospitals had posted files in a form no patient could interpret (CMS via Word & Brown, 2026; PatientRightsAdvocate, 2025). A rule that compels a generator to publish a verifier it has spent forty years suppressing produces, at first, the appearance of the verifier, which is what nine-digit placeholders in place of prices were until CMS closed that loophole in May 2025, and the rule drew ten civil penalties in 2025 (Word & Brown, 2026). Medicare's price is an administered price, the captured instance from Chapter 2, and it is copied: commercial contracts are commonly written as a multiple of the Medicare rate (RAND, 2024), so the state's number, set by a fee schedule and adjusted by lobbying, anchors the private number too. The one real price signal in the sector is the DRG, the activity-based tariff Medicare adopted in 1983 (Chapter 14), which verified that a hospital could deliver a discharge for a fixed sum and changed hospital behavior within a year, with Medicare length of stay down an estimated 7.6 percent attributable to the new payment in its first year (NBER, 1986; Annals of Internal Medicine, 2013); the tariff is a signal intervention, and the surrounding stack absorbed it.

Exit is disabled twice. The patient cannot leave the insurer without leaving the job, since the exclusion pays only for the employer's plan, and cannot leave the provider without leaving the network, whose prices she cannot see in order to choose within it. The employer can leave the insurer, and does, but the employer's exit verifies the premium, not the care, and the insurer's incentive under the medical loss ratio rule of 2010 is to keep spending high, because its permitted margin is capped as a share of a premium that rises with the claims it pays, 20 percent in the individual and small group market and 15 percent in the large group market (CMS, 2011). Election/press is live and noisy: Congress verifies the sector at intervals of a decade or two (in 1965, 1983, 2003, 2010, and 2020, the last being the No Surprises Act, enacted in December 2020 and effective in January 2022; Congressional Research Service, 2025) and each verdict adds a layer, because a legislature cannot select on a single output and can only add rules to the rules the previous verdict added. Bankruptcy is softened for institutions and imposed on patients. Benchmark is the one verifier that works, and it works where the axis says it should, at scale and on mortality. Medicare's published thirty-day mortality and readmission rates for named hospitals, and the penalty attached to excess readmissions since 2012 under the Hospital Readmissions Reduction Program, are a benchmark with a consequence, and readmission rates fell after the penalty attached (New England Journal of Medicine, 2016). Surgical quality sits at the medium calibration point (Chapter 3): a bad outcome is hard to tell from a bad patient one case at a time and easy to tell across ten thousand, so a verifier that aggregates works while every verifier that operates at the bedside fails. American wealth and continental scale raise the level of spending and explain nothing about why the patient cannot read a bill. The proof that the failure is intermediation and not the sector lives inside the same country. Where insurance does not reach, price reappears and does what price does. Laser eye surgery is paid in cash, advertised by price, and its real price has fallen while its quality has risen over two decades (Human Progress, 2024). The Surgery Center of Oklahoma posts all-inclusive prices for named procedures on its website and has done so since 2009 (Smith, 2013), at a fraction of the hospital chargemaster for the same procedure (Foundation for Economic Education, 2013), not because its surgeons are better but because a patient who can read the number can also refuse it. Cash-pay primary care, cosmetic surgery, and self-paid imaging show the same pattern. Same physicians, same licensing, same malpractice law, same state: the only variable that differs is whether a verifier stands between the patient and the generator, and where it does the sector behaves like a market, and where it does not it behaves the way a sector behaves under a ministry that sets a tariff and covers the deficit.

**Bindingness.** The consequences bind on the two parties who generate nothing, the patient and the employer. The patient bears the deductible, the balance bill, and the medical debt, which is the sector's bankruptcy verifier misdirected: bankruptcy is supposed to end the generator that produced the bad output, and here it ends the household that consumed it. The employer bears the premium and passes it to wages. The generators sit under a soft constraint (Kornai). The hospital's bad outcome is paid for a second time, since the readmission is a new admission, and only the Medicare penalty of 2012 attached any cost to it (Kaiser Family Foundation, 2018). The insurer's margin scales with spending. The pharmacy benefit manager's rebate scales with list price. The No Surprises Act, which took effect in January 2022 to protect patients from out-of-network balance bills, replaced the bill with an arbitration channel, and the channel shows what a verifier without a price looks like: about 590,000 disputes in the first half of 2024, 1.2 million in the first half of 2025, 4.8 million cases through the end of 2025, with providers winning 85 percent in 2024 and 88 percent in the first half of 2025, and median awards for some specialties of three to nine times the qualifying payment amount (Georgetown CHIR, 2026; CMS IDR reports). A process designed to shield the patient became a generator of awards to providers at a volume the state cannot process, which is the rule-thicket intervention of Chapter 4 producing complexity in the dark (Chapter 5). The administrative share, 34.2 percent of health spending in 2017 on the broadest definition against 17.0 percent in Canada on the same definition, and between 8 and 15 percent on definitions confined to billing and insurance (Himmelstein, Campbell, and Woolhandler, 2020), is that complexity measured.

The state's contribution to the softness is the part the case would be dishonest to omit, and it cuts against the operator and the structurer alike. The tax exclusion is the state paying the employer to keep the patient's exit disabled. Certificate-of-need laws, adopted under a federal mandate of 1974 that was repealed in 1986 and retained since by roughly two thirds of the states, 35 and the District of Columbia (Mercatus Center, 2021), are veto points in the exact sense of Chapter 4: a rival hospital or surgery center must obtain the state's permission to build, incumbents may object, and the ex ante block replaces the ex post verdict of exit. Medicare's administered price is copied downstream, so the state's captured verifier is everyone's. And the hospital that would fail is not permitted to, because disproportionate-share payments and the community's need for a hospital soften its constraint by expectation, which is Kornai's mechanism under a nonprofit charter. The market failure and the state failure here are not two failures; they are one loop with both hands on it. The symmetric state case is the NHS before the internal market of 1991 (the United Kingdom case, Chapter 10): a single public generator with no price, no exit, and a waiting list as its queue. The American sector reaches the same place from the private side, with the queue replaced by a bill nobody can read.

**The component.** The loop was opened by intermediation, not by ownership. Every intermediary in the stack is a generator whose output nobody prices: the employer's plan choice, the insurer's network, the benefit manager's formulary, the provider's negotiated rate, the coder's claim. Each is verified, if at all, by the generator above it, and the patient, the only party with an interest in the output, stands at the end of the chain with a bill in place of a verdict.
